  - Task 10 built the shared foundation: a control lease (`acquireControlLease`/`releaseControlLease`/`recordControlPatch`), scratch-artifact isolation for controls that re-run F1/F2's own detection logic (`createControlScratchSession`/`disposeControlScratchSession`), a generic two-tier-timeout Node-side wrapper (`runBridgedControl`), a `sessionTainted` short-circuit, and a Phase 11 cleanup fallback (`restoreAllControlPatches`).

  - Task 13 wired all six into `main()`'s real dependency object (`buildRealControls`) and added a cross-control staged test proving all six work correctly run back-to-back through one real `runHarness` sequence (lease non-overlap, scratch-session disposal, zero net drift in retained F1/F2/F4 artifacts), plus a mid-sequence-taint variant. Task 13's own review caught and fixed a real bug in already-"approved" Task 11 code (an unguarded property read that could leak a lease/scratch session) — a concrete example of why the dedicated final whole-pass review mattered.
  - **The dedicated final whole-pass review found one High-severity defect that defeated area 3's own stated purpose**: three controls (`wrong-owner`, `hidden-fragment`, `capacity-breach`) patched `model.fragList`/`model.tree` — bare properties that **do not exist on the real Viewer 7.124.2 Model class** (verified by downloading and decoding the actual pinned Viewer bundle, SHA-256-confirmed identical to `EXPECTED_VIEWER_SHA256`; fragments live in a private field exposed only via `getFragmentList()`, and no model class defines `.tree`). This meant all three controls would always return `NOT_EXECUTABLE` on a real run, making overall `PASS` structurally unreachable — three prior task reviews had checked only repo-wide usage CONVENTION (via grep) rather than the actual Viewer API surface, and mischaracterized this as a "future-stability" concern rather than a current defect. **This has now been fixed** (Task 14): all three controls use the correct accessor methods (`model.getFragmentList()`, `model.getObjectTree()`/`getInstanceTree()` fallback), the deviation from the original Node-side functions' own bare-property convention is documented in-code, and the tests that had been masking this bug (fixtures using a different object for the bare property vs. the accessor) were corrected to match the real production contract.
  - The same final review also found (and Task 14 also fixed) a related gap: an in-page-only detection timeout or restore failure did not taint the session the way an outer Node-side watchdog timeout did, risking a stale, still-running detection continuing to touch a live model after a later control had already proceeded. `runControlSafely` now taints the session on `TIMED_OUT`/`RESTORE_FAILED`/`restored===false` outcomes too, in addition to the outer-watchdog case.
  - Both fixes were independently re-verified by a scoped re-review (APPROVED, no further findings) before this pass was considered closed.

In both cases the implementer treated the output as unverified, independently re-confirmed every claim against source, and only acted on small, already-in-scope items (verified by the task's own reviewer in the second case).
- **Disclosed, NOT fixed this pass — open items for owner awareness / a future authorized fix wave (none of these can produce a false PASS; every one was independently traced through `computeOverall`'s unchanged precedence and confirmed still fail-closed):**
  1. **Generic control-exception doesn't taint the session** (new, found during Task 14's own fix-wave review, judged by that review as the higher-priority of two new residuals): if a control's own restore `finally` block itself throws (e.g. the shared liveness-check helper throwing), `fatalErrorObserved` is set (forcing the final verdict to FAIL) but `sessionTainted` is not — so a later step can still run against a possibly-still-perturbed model via this specific exception path, distinct from the resolved-`ControlResult` cases Task 14's own fix already covers.
  2. **`bounds-beyond-tolerance`'s combined "perturbation not representable + restore also failed" case is mislabeled** as `NOT_EXECUTABLE{executed:false}` rather than something the new taint check can see — same underlying gap as item 1, specific to this one control's own edge case.
  3. **`runCleanup`'s page/context/browser close steps have no bounded timeout** (pre-existing, predates this whole bounded pass — commit `41eeade`), unlike Task 9's own bounded `closeAllBestEffort` (which only covers the Phase 0 failure path). A wedged `page.close()` during normal (non-Phase-0) cleanup could still hang indefinitely.
  4. **The six original Node-side `run*Control` functions (and `runSetupThroughClassification`) are now confirmed dead code** — never called by the real wiring, superseded by the in-page ports, but left in place with increasingly stale doc comments. An earlier `codex-advisor` recommendation to delete them once parity was proven was adopted in the working ledger but not carried into the final task's own brief — worth a deliberate decision (delete vs. keep as historical reference) rather than further silent drift.
  5. **A Phase 0 evidence-handler edge case**: `handlePhase0Failure` is called without its own `try` — an unexpected throw inside it (not inside the code it's handling) could theoretically replace the primary Phase 0 error it's meant to report. Assessed as low-probability.
  6. **A scratch session can be leaked (page-memory-only, no live resource)** if a control is abandoned mid-flight via the lease-lost-mid-flight path — `restoreAllControlPatches`' own Phase 11 fallback covers lease/patch restoration but not this specific scratch-session cleanup.
- **Explicitly confirmed, per the authorization's own closing requirements:** live F1–F8 execution was NOT run at any point in this pass (the activation interlock — both `--execute-live-f1-f8` AND `G1_F1_F8_LIVE_AUTHORIZED=YES` required — independently re-verified unweakened by every task review AND the final whole-pass review); the one narrow real-browser allowance (Task 9's import-map acceptance test) was exercised only against a local synthetic page, never the real fixture/model; production `wwwroot/viewer.js`/`wwwroot/index.html` were only ever read, never modified; no model was uploaded/retranslated; G2/G3/H1/H2/OQ-1/host integration were NOT started; no Viewer Adapter/Core/M4/Panel production file was changed; nothing was pushed.

- **Independent findings (now remediated, see above):** **H1** the public Viewer API provides no per-node theming readback, so clearing Panel theming can erase foreign theming and cannot meet the claimed rollback-safe ownership contract; minimum safe v1 correction is markers-only or an explicit enforceable exclusive-theming precondition. **H2** Soft remains enabled when model unit scales differ even though the design calls its distances mixed native units; require a verified common world/unit basis and fail closed otherwise. **M1** remove registration from the Panel-core port and keep registration/OQ-1/unload composition in the host boundary. **L1** add `derivations.filter` to the §0 minimum API summary.

The restricted first attempt had only the known three local-port `EPERM` failures and was superseded by the passing run.
- **Major design decisions (spec §3, PD-1…PD-14):** new Panel layer `clashpanel/` above `clashtest/` + `clashtest/viewer/` (headless core + ViewerBridge + thin view; layering guard); v1 modes = Hard (`volume-overlap`) + Soft (`clearance`); all filtering Panel-side over sealed `results[]`; Group-by-Element = Core `element` facet once per run + filter mask (presentation-only); pagination over an index-based ordered list with occurrence identity; results browsable **only after seal** (Core exposes only `resultsFound` during a run); staleness by provenance binding generation/version, never by digests; one ownership-guarded visualization session with a serialized, write-ahead-ledger presentation transaction; isolation enabled only when read-and-compare is reliable and nothing is hidden (no clear-all fallback); no bare "No clashes"; no ETA/telemetry; residency via one-shot leases (admission-first).
- **Architecture disposition:** layering, sealed-result browsing, pagination, presentation-only grouping, generation/version-based reload safety, isolation ownership, and Core/Adapter separation are sound. The reload-digest discrepancy is not a Panel prerequisite. **No Viewer Adapter extension is currently required**; F1–F8 verification may expose a defect but does not pre-authorize one. OQ-1 remains an integration-acceptance prerequisite, not a headless-design dependency.
- **Owner decisions:** OD-1–OD-4, OD-6–OD-12 and OD-14 are genuine product/design choices. **OD-5 (reframed):** accept markers-only emphasis as v1; theming only under a separate enforceable exclusive-theming contract. **OD-13 (reframed):** Part 1 computation validity — accept fail-closed Soft and the host-evidence dependency (or defer Soft); Part 2 unit wording only afterwards. **No new owner decision was introduced by the DockingPanel/UX changes (owner direction).** OD-8 does not settle OQ-1 and remains an integration policy decision.

R-1 is resolved: the waiter is queued before caller-controlled registration; reentrant grants are retained until registration settles; registration failure removes the failing waiter and releases any temporarily granted slot; synchronous abort, nested acquire and FIFO/capacity accounting remain consistent. Independent verification: listener-failure files **21/21**; affected suites **692/692**; full suite **2,619 total / 2,618 pass / 0 fail / 1 pre-existing todo**. Throwing listener methods fail closed (no leak, no stranded waiter; pinned by the M-1/R-1 regressions). Hostile signal-like objects whose `addEventListener`/`removeEventListener` synchronously re-enter the read coordinator or release unrelated slots are **outside the supported contract**: **settlement order under such reentrancy is undefined** (R-2: with `maxConcurrentStoreReads ≥ 2`, a later waiter can settle first). Independently confirmed for R-2: no slot leak, stranded waiter, capacity corruption or false result occurs. A second, independently-constructed **duplicate-masking variant** (weights honestly sum to `pairSpaceTotal`, but a duplicate leaf masks one genuinely-missing required pair) also validates clean — this independently confirms the task's own reasoning for rejecting the "check weights against `pairSpaceTotal`" fallback option, since that option would not catch this variant either.

**There is a genuine, unresolved tension in your own ruling text, surfaced by independent verification, not resolved here — needs your disambiguation:** the ruling's opening sentence ("exactly satisfies the required question") reads as pair-level against frozen spec §6.1's own definition of the required question as the pair set P; the itemized bullet list is phrased at root level (which REM7 fully satisfies); but one bullet in that same list — "duplicated/overlapping ranges must not substitute for missing required coverage" — independently confirmed to point back toward pair-level, since the duplicate-masking variant above is exactly an overlapping range substituting for a missing pair-level gap, and it is not caught.

**Owner's options for candidate I** (from the task report, independently sanity-checked): (a) accept root/partner-class coverage as P5's closure, defer exact pair-level cover as its own tracked gate; (b) weight/`pairSpaceTotal` arithmetic — independently confirmed insufficient (doesn't catch the duplicate-masking variant); (c) persist explicit topology evidence — a schema change; (d) a canonical-layout verifier — needs a ruling on whether §6.3's block-triangular alternative layout stays legal, since ledger records carry no cell/orientation metadata; (e, found by independent verification, not in the original list) a closed-form `|P|` count plus a rectangle/interval-union coverage proof over evaluated leaves — computationally tractable (the task's "infeasible" framing was independently found somewhat overstated for an exact check in general, though correct for naive pair enumeration specifically), but still a new validation mechanism requiring its own design ruling.

**Two further informational findings (J, K), independently spot-checked plausible, not this round's scope:** J — `status.js`'s pair-status lookup assumes exactly one leaf covers a pair (matches its own doc comment's §6.3 reliance), so once duplicates are accepted (candidate I), pair status can depend on list order; only matters once I is addressed. K — spec §6.6's "empty operand → `coverage:'full'` with zero units" is contradicted by both the writer's and validator's own `>0 leaves` requirement, but this fails safe (never claimable either way) and pre-dates this session.

For each of the 4 residual items it gives the exact current behavior (file:line), the governing frozen spec/plan clause(s), the concrete failure mode, a classification (plan gap / cross-phase issue / unresolved ambiguity — none is a P5 code bug), the smallest viable options with their scope/cost and which later phases/interfaces each touches, and a clearly-labeled recommendation. It performs no new review and changes no code/test/spec/plan file. Item 3 (the `deriveRunOutcome`/`freshnessStateOf` question) is recorded there as a **disposition**, not something needing an owner decision — a second-model verification confirmed it is already correctly assigned to P6 by the existing plan text, and caught the controller's own first-pass proposed "fix" as an unauthorized deviation before it was ever applied.
- **A plan erratum found and NOT self-corrected (reported here for the controller/owner):** `p5-result-set.md`'s own Task P5.9 Step 1 code block asserts `assert.equal(JSON.stringify(read.results), JSON.stringify(sealed.results), kind)`. This is unconditionally false for any non-empty `results[]`: `InMemoryResultStore.put()` persists `canonicalJson(resultSet)` (`util/canonical.js`), which sorts every object's keys by UTF-16 code-unit order, and `readResultSet`/`captureData` (`util/capture.js`) deliberately preserve the input's own key order rather than restoring the producer's insertion order (`resultId, pair, mode, evaluatedAs, evidence, flags, unitId`, per `projectUnitPairs`) — so the two `JSON.stringify` calls differ in key order alone even though the objects are semantically identical. Verified against a second model (`codex-advisor`, 2026-09-22, request logged this session) before treating it as settled, not guessed. Per this repository's `CLAUDE.md` freeze-respect rule, the shipped test (`test/clashtest/results/roundTrip.test.mjs`) replaces that one line with `assert.deepStrictEqual(read.results, sealed.results, kind)` — same content-preservation property, insensitive to the store's documented canonical-JSON mechanism — but the governing plan artifact itself still carries the original, disprovable assertion and was **not** edited (this session made no plan/spec edit). **Other plan errata found this session, also not self-corrected, also for the controller/owner to fold in together:** the new `duplicate-owner-dbid` issue code (P5.5) and the new `InvalidOptionsError{code:'already-started'}` (P5.8) are not named in the plan's issue-code tables; the `ScopeProof`/`Digests` `digestVersion` ambiguity (item 6 above); `admitSealedSet`'s own prose (already correct) vs. its pre-fix code (now corrected to match) no longer disagree, but the plan's own reference code at that section still shows the old, incomplete check. The controller/owner should correct `p5-result-set.md` to match, in one pass, once a ruling is made on items 3–6 above (some of these errata are downstream of those rulings).
- **Next authorized action:** targeted adjudication of the P5 remediation focused re-review at `eb48d5c`. Do not modify production code during adjudication. The five unrelated findings require separate owner disposition. **P6 must not start before P5 acceptance plus separate explicit owner authorization.**
- **Carry-forwards (unchanged unless noted):** P6's `factsFromOutcome` must implement independent death detection via a live-binding boundary scan (in addition to CF4 `outcome.kind`→`outcome.outcome` and CF5 reading `outcome.generations`, both still open) and implement `stagePlanned`/`commitPlanned`; H2's four unsliced spans (extended CF3) pending AC-12/G-CAL; H3's `dedupMap` working-set bound folded into R11-7/G-CAL. **New this session:** P6, when it integrates the canonical `deriveRunOutcome` path, must eliminate or formally reconcile `validateResultSet.js`'s hand-duplicated coverage/integrity/environment-sensitive derivation logic in `checkCrossField` (currently a manual copy of `resultSet.js`'s logic, not a shared-function call — owner-deferred from the P5 remediation adjudication's M1 drift-risk observation). **Unchanged non-blocking items:** the inert `checkpoint(label)` argument; roots-observation row shape; P3 L-R1 eager-helper target-list disposition. **Resolved this session:** the `taints[]` schema ceiling reconciliation item (landed in P5.5, `resultSetSchema.js`'s `MAX_UNIT_TAINTS`) is no longer open; the P5 plan's missing `definition-not-normalized` rule-table row is no longer open (fixed, uncommitted).
